# One value printer: census, rules, enforcement and conversion slices

> **Slices moved 2026-10-08.** The ordered slices of this audit (P01-P08, E01-E04, Appendix A/B) are rows `PR-` of [the agent-loop ledger](../../../../seon/schedule/agent-loop-ledger.md) §3.3, deduplicated against the Opus printer rules and the message audits. The census and the fifteen rules stay as evidence.

Seon already selects a node tree in `seon.render.value` and emits its AI and HTML faces in `seon.print`. The smallest design is to finish that composition: immutable values → optional render data → one budgeted selection → two emissions. Delete outside display allocation and printed logical copies; retain parsing, explicit query limits and necessary wire encodings.

This is a design and static audit, not a printer implementation or an installation claim. Owner instruction of 2026-10-08 and CLAUDE.md's complete Presentation section govern it. The older clipping survey's recommendation to delete the token budget is superseded: **one budget belongs inside the printer**. The October 7 inventory's exemptions for small scalar messages are also too broad for this instruction: values embedded in error prose belong in data members regardless of size.

## Evidence and counting

Lane: `printer-audit`. Only this document is owned. Claimed with `bin/lane claim` before creation. Source was read in the shared checkout on 2026-10-08; the per-file SHA-256 manifest below pins the actual contents used for the census. HEAD advanced during the audit; the manifest, not a claim of an atomic checkout, identifies the inspected bytes. Default was running archive `c98ade40426dba469db4f0b8bf05156206d66753` at the initial status check. No definitions, source loads, reloads, restarts, provider calls or foreign-lane messages were made.

A **site** in the counts is a distinct physical source line containing an operation in the census, not the number of calls on that line. A line can contain multiple operations and is counted once; a multi-line expression's operative lines can count separately. The ledger lists every anchor with its containing function, class, consumer, justification and actual source fragment. This makes the large counts reproducible rather than presenting them as a count of independent defects. This includes helper definitions as well as callers; deleting a helper can remove several sites. Class (a) includes unbounded display conversion and display-budget allocation, not just substring cuts. Class (b) includes conversion to text for ordering, storage, identity or sizing a stored copy. Class (c) is a reviewed non-display use or an output sink of text already produced by the printer.

Scope: `src/`, `script/` (including its test/report scripts), Babashka executables in `bin/`, `bin/seon-hook`, `.clj-kondo/config.edn`, its assertion hook, `.claude/seon-hook.edn`, and both sides of the MCP bridge. Shell launchers `bin/mcp-server`, `bin/seon`, `bin/test`, `bin/lane`, `bin/lint` were read/searched: their argument forwarding and literal shell diagnostics are transport/CLI control, not Clojure value printers; the relevant returned-value formatting is in the BB/JVM owners listed below. The wider `test/` tree was linted as requested, but its ordinary fixture text is not part of the production census. Production test reporting in `src/seon/test*` is included.

Search covered bare and qualified printers, `with-out-str`, pprint, `str`/format, Java string/writer methods, `subs`, `subvec`, `take`/`take-last`, printed sorting, custom print methods, stack construction, fixed limits and elision markers. Comments and string literals were masked before collecting syntax candidates. Reviewed the October 7 inventory by matching **current source text**, not importing its line numbers; expanded it with current `str` and hook/bridge sites. Query/display `count` uses and named budget helpers were read in their surrounding producers. Constants, declarations, quoted examples, Datalog `...`, parser spans and storage/execution bounds are not automatically violations.

Static limitation: this is a census of current first-party source, not a proof about arbitrary runtime macro expansion or dynamically resolved third-party functions. Some source lines contain both an encoder and a display caller; the ledger class names the operation described, and the encoder itself is separately classified. Public behavior, branch installation and every proposed regression below remain to be proven by the implementation slices. No candidate is silently declared safe because its name contains “bounded” or “canonical.”

### Findings that matter first

1. **The edit hook cheats on its own rule.** `bin/seon-hook:1083` `findings-feedback` takes an advisory `subvec` and adds an omitted count; `:1215` `clip-feedback` makes another token-to-character prefix; `:1421` `clip-with-marker` cuts source/rubric bytes with a warning. `:2036` captures a complete Java stack and logs it. Review is currently disabled, but these are executable paths, not permission to retain them. Markdown/docstring formatting adds two more independent clippers (`script/seon/dev/markdown.clj:1210`, `docstring.clj:355`).
2. **Error recording maintains another printer/allocation algorithm.** `src/seon/error.clj:356` `bounded-text`, `:451` `fit-fact-payload` divide a storage quota across printed strings, reprint facts and repeatedly halve it. `seon.sci.admit` constructs print-node EDN and `seon.id/printed` owns a second generic printer. The errors spec already requires stored observation to be the printer's exact shown text plus references. Do not turn this into a third “safe serializer.”
3. **The test runner subdivides the budget itself.** `src/seon/test/runner.clj:118` `message-text` compares characters with tokens, and `:155` `report-pair` grants separate expected/actual allocations. Pass one report map to the printer. `test/accretion.clj:111` takes three failures and builds “more” prose; `test.clj:1712` cuts the offending identities before the printer sees them. `bin/test-check:101` prints every cause's ex-data unbounded.
4. **Pages and renders still produce value text.** The web debug surfaces use raw `pr-str` (`render/web.clj:752,794,802,982,3290,3292,3294,3296`). REPL directory HTML uses `str` on arglists (`repl.clj:54`), source layout uses pprint (`:107`), and many schema/error/config/model/agent renderers interpolate values. `my/program.clj:866` destroys source after 97 characters. `problems.clj:450` shortens digests to eight characters for display.
5. **Generic presentation widths leak into acquisition.** `render/walk.clj:299,475,1258`, `render/schema.clj:65,68`, `render/var.clj:123`, `render/transcript.clj:847` and `health.clj:665` choose visible prefixes outside the printer. A database query page or a genuine top-N answer may be bounded; taking a prefix because it must fit a view is not that case.
6. **Printed copies survive structural hashing.** `seon.id/digest` now hashes structure; do not repeat the old claim that all identities still hash EDN. Remaining independent offenders include `script/seon/dev/dependency_digest.clj:145`, `script/seon/operator.clj:586`, graph ids (`render/web.clj:853,870`), contract path ids (`program.cljc:766`) and text fallback comparison (`schema/datahike.clj:525`). Program contract/arglist/default/label strings, background result references and AI attempt settings/usage are logical printed copies, not display output.

### Non-display boundaries that must survive

- A `subs` with parser/source-span offsets is not a display clip: `sci/reader`, `sci/forgive`, `seon.edit`, `seon.fn`, schema resource paths and patch reconstruction retain their semantics. Agent-requested file/edit windows (`fs/jvm.clj:519`, `edit.clj:59`) define the requested value and carry offsets; they must not masquerade as whole files.
- Whole-prompt-unit selection (`cluster/prompt.clj:280`) admits complete historical units. It must never substring their saved shown text. Selecting whole review units in `bin/seon-hook:1480` is the same kind of operation; clipping the selected unit at `:1425` is not.
- Explicit query limits (`my/program.clj:889`, `web/search.clj:27`), algorithmic ambiguity detection (`call_preparation.clj:966,998`), stored-validation sample/work limits (`db.clj:4065,4071`), schema candidate search limits and profiling top-N requests are separate from display. Declare whether an answer is complete and supply a next query when it is a page. Do not fabricate a total from `limit+1`.
- The SCI output writer (`sci/eval.clj:4053`) refuses the next write at a storage bound rather than silently shortening it. Its `subs` at `:4077` is the Writer's requested `(offset,length)` chunk. Preserve captured output exactly, record the refusal, and let the printer select the visible `:out` value. Changing this to silently accept a prefix would create a new class-(a) defect.
- JVM/SCI session print-length/level values are **user-owned requests**, carried into selection (`turn/batch.clj:931`, `sci/eval.clj:4227`). Carrying them is legitimate. A helper binding these Vars to hide platform output is not. The printer may honor user bounds only through its normal cut nodes and accounting.
- EDN quoting of a JSON request (`script/seon/dev/mcp.clj:77`), route lookup refs, percent escaping, JSON-RPC, exact source sent for evaluation and atomic machine-readable operator records are transport. They must round-trip and remain local to their paired reader. Physical blob/attribute encoding is likewise a dependency codec, not a second display implementation. Logical fields such as `settings-edn` storing a printed copy of data are class (b), even though their name advertises EDN.
- HTML escaping, CSS tokens and DOM identifiers are final grammar encoding, not value selection. Already-selected text can be escaped without a second budget. Arbitrary maps in a Hiccup child are not an excuse to call `str`.
- Removing an exactly shared stack suffix (`error/refusal.clj:174`) is structural deduplication, not a visible quota. Its enclosing link must preserve the omitted information. Dropping frames because they lack a filename is a separate evidence-loss concern, not licensed by this classification. `Throwable->map` and complete trace presentation still need conversion.
- Log rotation (`maintenance.clj:246`, file truncation `bin/seon-hook:85`), HTTP body limits and deadlines bound resources. They must not be changed to printer budgets. Native namespace/path/UUID/hex construction is not a printed copy of an arbitrary value; textual sorting of values is class (b).

## General printer contract — fifteen rules

1. Preserve the actual value and result handle; presentation never mutates or replaces them.
2. Take one explicit profile and one budget for the complete displayed unit, including labels, delimiters, cuts and diagnostics.
3. Select a structural node tree once; AI text and HTML use that same selection and cut identities.
4. Reserve affordable root/cut/error structure, then share remaining space across siblings before expanding deeper values.
5. Charge escaped output and layout as emitted; never print everything first, binary-search a quota or substring final output.
6. Treat strings as complete text values: Unicode-safe spans, lines where possible, with a continuation for every hidden span.
7. Treat source as a general code/text presentation kind; ordinary definitions fit whole and preserve their source, without symbol-specific rules.
8. Treat `:out` as already-captured text, included in the same envelope budget; capture/storage refusal is separate from display selection.
9. Normalize a Throwable to compact error data—type, message, operation when known, references, cause links—not a stack-trace string.
10. Render exactly once; render output is data, never implicitly evaluated or offered to dispatch again.
11. Traverse only what selection needs; never `count`, hash, sort, print or realize an unknown tail merely to describe a cut.
12. Use identity-aware cycle/reference nodes and an iterative traversal; never dereference host objects to discover their contents.
13. Every cut carries its path, bound, unit, continuation and exact cut identity; known finite omitted counts are exact, unknown tails say unknown.
14. With no database, use the same core/profile and a supplied live handle or explicit unavailable-retrieval data; no ambient store or fake runnable handle.
15. Renders may choose meaningful fields, summaries and requery data; only the printer may choose a quota-driven visible prefix.

### Allocation, text and continuation details

Input is a value, optional already-selected render data, explicit immutable world/profile and retrieval basis. Output is a declared projection containing the selected tree, one cut ledger, diagnostics, AI text and Hiccup. These are extensions of the existing projection, not a new printer API or registry. Validate at the producer with Malli `validator`/`explain` (pinned `43c6dca8d2`, `reference-code/malli/src/malli/core.cljc:2672,2705`), then compile the contract with its actual consumers.

The unit boundary matters: expected/actual/message is one report; result/out/findings/diagnostics is one reply; an error and its cause summary is one error unit. Per-key calls to `core-text` do not constitute one budget. Whole historical units may be selected for a prompt without charging or clipping their text again. A larger containing page may lay out those immutable artifacts, but cannot reprint each artifact under a fresh quota.

Use the existing breadth-first selection with cheap scalar summaries, bounded local ordering and whole-node admission. Budget accounting includes cut syntax and retrieval forms, not just the visible prefix. A tiny budget either shows an affordable reference with a compact diagnostic or reports a typed insufficient-budget outcome beside the retained value; it must not enter an arbitrary 8192-character fallback. Bound the profile's valid minimum at entry. Diagnostic count may be summarized, but the ledger remains available through the same handle. If a renderer fails, the original value and one path-carrying diagnostic enter the same allocation, with rendering disabled for that value.

Strings preserve newlines, tabs and code points. A UTF-16 offset must not bisect a surrogate pair; record the indexing unit. Prefer the last complete line that fits; if a single line is too long, cut at a safe code-point boundary and name the continuation offset. Preserve quotation/escaping for ordinary strings and source spelling for a declared code presentation. Never turn every source line into independently budgeted strings. Neither a field named `:source` nor `:out` may trigger a domain rule in the printer: the entrant supplies a general text/code node with its retrieval relation. HTML may style it, but cannot select more or less text.

A cut ledger counts **cut nodes**, exactly once, not every hidden descendant that was never visited. For a vector or string with a known size, `omitted = total - offset - shown`; the tree and both faces agree. An uncounted lazy tail has `count-status :unknown` plus an exact shown count and next offset, never a fictitious `+N`. Cycles are reference nodes, not infinitely many omitted children. Exact omitted counts for arbitrary infinite sequences are mathematically unavailable; this is not a reason to traverse them. When a finite tail is later exhausted, the next projection may report its exact count.

Continuations must be data forms grounded in the **actual retained value**. Vector indices, sequence steps, map keys (including non-keyword keys), set members and text spans need typed paths; plain `get-in` does not index a list. `seon.print/value-at` is the existing path entrance to improve. Preserve snapshot identity across database pages. A rendered projection differs from its source value: retain the projection with the result's runtime owner or offer an explicit read-only render/requery operation tied to its basis; never claim `get-in original [:rendered-key]` retrieves it. The renderer's offered form remains data until the agent explicitly evaluates it.

A continuation for captured output reads the captured output, not the result value. A gone result answers a typed gone value with saved shown text, not a recomputation of effects. Off custody, a caller may pass a retrieval function/form backed by its own retained value; if it cannot retain it, every cut must explicitly say retrieval is unavailable. The current `core` symbol is not a universally executable retrieval location.

Finite lazy sequences may be stepped only for the admitted prefix; chunked Clojure sequences may realize a dependency chunk, so do not promise exactly one producer call per shown item. Use an unchunked generator for the deterministic work regression. A host iterator or lazy step may block in host code: the printer cannot guarantee termination of arbitrary host calls. Opaque host objects are shown by class/identity; execution ownership and exit monitoring belong to the entrant, without another hidden printer thread pool.

### Errors, renders, and what the printer must not do

Error producers build fixed messages and native members, throwing/returning the installed error shape as appropriate. The error owner captures cause relationships and references; the printer selects compact data from that general Throwable representation. It must not inspect a database, future, atom or context by dereferencing it, call hostile `toString`/custom `print-method`, or expand all frames to discover which ones to hide. A compact stack/location projection may be requested explicitly; frame count and shared-tail information stay data.

Render selection remains value override → viewing namespace's fitting renderer → schema pair by specificity → raw value. Renderers may project meaningful fields, counts, identities and operations. They may choose an explicit domain answer such as “largest N functions” when N is the query's semantics; they may not add `take 5` because the page looks crowded or a model budget is small. They may call a nested render deliberately, but cannot give it an independent print budget. They receive a database value, never a connection/writer; no task scheduling, evaluation, settlement, publication or effect belongs in rendering. Cache only content-valid work, including viewer/face/profile/renderer identity and observed reads. Do not hash an infinite input to obtain a cache key: uncachable values remain uncached.

The printer must not: execute offered forms; impose schema-specific field order or summaries; truncate accepted values; create stored logical copies; compute identity by printing; silently catch and erase a failure; allocate separate diagnostic/string budgets; sort or traverse an entire unknown collection; rerender a saved historical unit; use HTML as an unbounded escape hatch; or make `seon.print/full` an unbounded route for an infinite value. Explicit expansion requests select a page or a separately declared bounded export, retaining truthful cuts.

### Installed gaps versus the target

Source evidence, not claims that these branches were exercised:

- `render/value.clj:86-124` still has `text-limits`, `datum` and character truncation in the fallback inside the allowed printer files. Moving bad code inside the whitelist is insufficient: the internal fallback must obey the same selection/cut contract.
- `:1544-1562` `plain-projection` prints a separate 8192-character value and a separately bounded diagnostic. `:1637-1664` appends diagnostics outside the value's charge. These are first-priority printer fixes.
- `:1617-1621` full mode removes budget/depth/child bounds. `:1002-1038` still obtains schema identity projection and related data inside printer selection. Move semantic acquisition into the render owner; the printer receives generic nodes and references.
- `:1061-1089` raw renderer text and projected render data need reliable distinct retrieval roots. `:1583-1615` already makes one selection for both faces; preserve this rather than adding another HTML allocator.
- `repl.clj:105-107` owns a second code layout entry. `test/runner.clj:155-174` owns another allocator. Both should become ordinary calls with one complete value.
- `seon.render.lint` is absent from current source. `render/value.clj:282` still mentions it in a docstring: stale evidence, not an installed check.

Prior art read locally at the pinned revisions: Fipp `255a19061f`, `edn.cljc:9-21` separates collection documents from emission but its independent print-length ellipsis has no Seon retrieval; `visit.cljc:32-52` and `engine.cljc:10-46` provide the general node/emission seam. nREPL `f3a044153e`, `QuotaBoundWriter.java:26-37` writes a prefix then throws, so it is unsuitable for Seon's displayed value selection. Clojure `3bc2b3e91f`, `core_print.clj:484-501` walks Throwable causes without the needed identity-cycle protection. Reuse the existing Seon tree/emit-both and error owner rather than import a quota writer or build another visitor. Work should be O(selected nodes + emitted text + reached render reads), with auxiliary memory proportional to those nodes and traversal depth; no whole-value preprint. New derivation is needed only when the value/render input, observed read, profile, viewer or printer changes.

## Enforcement: a refusal, not an advisory

The existing clj-kondo `:discouraged-var` list (`.clj-kondo/config.edn:50-73`) covers bare printers and pprint, **at warning level**. Its namespace exceptions (`:109-111`) include `seon.id`, contradicting the law's two owners. The assertion hook (`.clj-kondo/hooks/is_message.clj:9-35`) is error-level, but only inspects the third child of `is`, with a literal symbol list; it misses `str`, aliases of pprint and helper calls. `bin/seon-hook:932` refuses error-level findings only. Post-edit refusal at `:1160` is syntax-specific. The hook's extension-based Clojure detection and lane lint roots also omit extensionless BB scripts from the ordinary `src script test` check. Independently, `.clj-kondo/config.edn:126` filters reported filenames to `.clj/.cljc/.cljs/.edn`: explicit stdin analysis of `bin/seon-hook` and `bin/test-check` resolved their printer usages but returned zero findings (93 ms and 17 ms). Enforcement must remove that output-filter blind spot as well as add BB file discovery.

Observed fixture, 13 ms: plain `pr-str` and aliased `pp/write` each emitted one warning. `with-out-str` around Writer.write, `subs`/`count`, `take-last`, `str` interpolation, `…`/more counts, print-length/level binding, Java `printStackTrace`, `sort-by str`, and `is` with `(str x)` emitted **no printer finding**. Only `(is ... (pr-str x))` was refused by the assertion hook. The fixture used namespace `printer-audit.fixture`, filename `printer_audit/fixture.clj`; an initial filename mismatch was corrected before recording these results.

The captured-source lint pass checked 119 files in 9,264 ms: 0 errors, 650 warnings, 7 info. Of 1,053 violating ledger anchors, **145 received only a warning and 908 had no printer finding; none was refused**. The 908 misses are explicitly marked `none` on every class-(a)/(b) ledger row. The counts split as (a) 96 warnings / 796 misses, (b) 49 warnings / 112 misses. Another 37 class-(c) anchors received printer warnings, demonstrating why a blanket token ban needs narrow transport/literal exceptions. The explicit BB probes above supplement the directory scan, which skipped their extensionless filenames. This second scan was needed to associate findings with the frozen census bytes after other lanes moved source lines; it is not a repeat claim about the changing working tree.

Required implementation: one static rule family in the existing analyzer/hook path, reused by prospective edit checks, source admission and `bin/lane`'s candidate check. Do not invent `seon.render.lint` as a second renderer. Use resolved clj-kondo usages plus the source AST to inspect argument positions and bindings. Emit error data containing rule, resolved callee, containing definition, source span, sink and a suggested data replacement. A diagnostic says **where the forbidden conversion occurs**; it does not print the offending runtime value.

| Rule | Exact refusal criterion outside `seon.print` / `seon.render.value` | Narrow allowed case |
|---|---|---|
| P1 bare printer | Resolved reference or call to `pr`, `prn`, `print`, `println`, `printf`, `pr-str`, `prn-str`, `print-str`, `println-str`, pprint `write/pprint`, `print-method`, `print-dup`; include higher-order uses and Var aliases. | Literal-only prose sink; writing a printer-produced text artifact unchanged; audited codec call paired with an actual reader. No namespace-wide `seon.id` exception. |
| P2 capture/alternate writer | `with-out-str`/StringWriter/StringBuilder receiving arbitrary values via print, `.append(Object)`, `.write`, `toString`, `String/valueOf`, `format %s`; a helper summary propagates the same conversion effect to callers. | Capturing already textual external output or constructing parser/transport bytes; no visible quota. |
| P3 text interpolation | `str`, `format`, `str/join`, Java format/append or equivalent with a nonliteral operand reaching a display sink: error message/fix, log argument, MCP text content, HTTP body/Hiccup text child, test report/assertion message, render output. Track locals, destructuring, return values and higher-order map/comp. | Literal concatenation; native path/name/UUID construction used as data; route/protocol fields with paired decoding. Value formatting for numeric/date display belongs inside the printer too. |
| P4 cut/budget | `subs`, `substring`, `take`, `take-last`, `subvec`, prefix loops, count/min/max deciding a prefix or `max-chars`/`max-bytes`/token estimate controlling display; refuse also a cut of printer-produced text or HTML. | Source/parser spans, actual requested file window, explicit query/work page, complete prompt-unit admission, deadline/resource refusal and log rotation; prove their distinct output contract. A renderer taking “shown-tests” is not a query exemption. |
| P5 invented elision | Nonliteral omitted-count arithmetic combined with ellipsis/“more”/“elided”/truncation notice, or construction of printer elision nodes outside the two owners. | Literal prose containing ellipsis, Datalog syntax, parser grammar, or forwarding an unchanged printer cut node. A query continuation is query data, not a fabricated printer cut. |
| P6 print globals | `binding`, `with-bindings`, `set!`, `alter-var-root` or SCI equivalent targeting print-length/level to constrain platform output. | Entrant captures/restores an agent's explicitly requested session state and passes it to the one printer. Fixture source testing that request is allowed in its declared regression. |
| P7 identity/copy | Text from a printer/`str` reaches digest/hash, comparator/sort, logical persistence member or copy-bearing message; refuse `sort-by str`, compare of printed data and preprint-to-measure. | Hashing actual authored source bytes; exact machine encoding at the storage/transport boundary; sorting already textual user strings. Symbols/keywords compare as native values or namespace/name tuples. |
| P8 stacks/errors | `.printStackTrace`, `Throwable->map` for display, stack-string capture or stack prefix filtering to fit output; dynamic values embedded in `ex-info`/error message prose. | Error-owner capture of structural cause links/first authored location/shared-tail refs; printer selects visible frames. Preserve evidence; no “frames take 10” exception. |
| P9 renders/envelopes | Renderer output passed to another print call or fresh profile budget; render code executes its offered form, writes, or supplies a connection; caller clips a saved shown artifact. | Explicit composition of nested render **data**, read-only requery metadata and whole-unit prompt selection. |

This cannot be made sound by searching only for function names: `let`, aliases, helper wrappers and macro expansion can hide them. Use conservative interprocedural summaries for known first-party helpers; unresolved/dynamic conversion into a display sink requires a reviewed typed boundary, not an automatic pass. Names like `short-text` or `safe-value` confer no exemption. Neither does a `#_:clj-kondo/ignore` next to the expression: printer-rule suppressions must be refused by the lane/admission check unless they identify an independently validated class-(c) boundary.

An allowed boundary is an exact call-site/symbol plus declared input/output role and a test naming the actual reader or query semantics. Its body must not forward its encoding to a display sink. It is invalidated on source/contract changes and is inspected by the same rule. This is not an author-controlled `:printer-safe true` flag or a filename allowlist. Admit runtime source through the same source analysis; a shell edit bypassing PreToolUse still fails PostToolUse/admission and the committed candidate gate. Include executable BB files by shebang, not extension, and lint the hook itself. Comments and data literals are ignored unless the existing source-to-evaluation boundary actually executes them. Test messages accept literal prose or data that the runner owns; no wholesale test-directory exception.

## Ordered implementation slices

All names below are **proposed proof tests**, not assertions that a test or target API already exists. Each slice is a roughly 15-minute implementation/probe checkpoint; schema migration, adoption or a failing dependency can require further checkpoints. Never claim that a schema-storage migration is a fifteen-minute mechanical sweep. The table in the appendix partitions every class-(a)/(b) producer into explicit file-owned conversion checkpoints. No slice edits a foreign-owned file; shared proof files serialize those landings.

Every proof enters `seon.mcp/call` with JSON `eval_clj`, explicit cluster/agent on a disposable test world, as `test/seon/mcp_entrance_test.clj:24` already does. Return small assertion summaries as data so the reply is fully readable; inspect a retained result in a second MCP evaluation for large values. `support/agent-value` alone does not prove the MCP boundary. Assert original values, projection trees/cut data, diagnostic variants, durable facts and continuation results. Do not regex-match a CLI report. BB/CLI proofs run the script in the fixture's disposable root, retrieve its declared result data through MCP, and assert it; a static rule proof also runs the same source-admission owner through MCP. No tests redefine production Vars or load working-tree source into default.

| Order | Files and change | Proposed proof in `seon.mcp-entrance-test` (all DATA through MCP) |
|---|---|---|
| P01 | `src/seon/render/value.clj`, its schema EDN, proof file: unify failure/diagnostic allocation with normal selection; remove `plain-projection`'s independent quotas. | `one-printer-budget-includes-diagnostics`: deliberately failing render preserves original value, emits one diagnostic/cut ledger and stays within the projection's declared charge. |
| P02 | `src/seon/print.cljc`, `render/value.clj`, their schema EDN, proof file: finish typed paths and exact cut ledger. | `every-printer-cut-can-be-followed`: vector/list/lazy-seq/map/set/string table; execute each offered continuation via a second MCP call and compare the tail data. |
| P03 | Same printer owners and proof file: shared string/source/code selection; Unicode-safe long-line continuation. | `text-cuts-preserve-source-and-unicode`: full retained source unchanged; prefix plus retrieved suffix reconstructs the text; offsets never split a surrogate pair. |
| P04 | Same printer owners, `error/refusal.clj` only if structural Throwable projection needs it, proof file: compact error nodes and hostile-object safety. | `throwables-are-compact-data`: nested errors/cause cycle returns class/type/message/reference nodes and preserves access to held evidence, without invoking custom toString. |
| P05 | Same printer owners and proof file: off-custody inputs and retrieval refusal; remove fake core handles and unlimited full traversal. | `off-custody-cuts-name-real-retrieval`: same tree with explicit profile and no db; valid supplied handle retrieves, absent holder gives typed unavailable data. |
| P06 | Printer owners, `src/seon/render.clj`, proof file: semantic acquisition leaves printer; nested render data does not redispatch. | `render-data-is-selected-once`: renderer invocation count is one; nested render-looking maps stay data; AI/HTML cut ids agree. |
| P07 | `src/seon/repl.clj`, `src/seon/sci/eval.clj`, `src/seon/turn/batch.clj`, relevant schema EDN, proof file: whole reply value/out/findings allocation, captured output retained separately. | `reply-output-shares-the-value-budget`: complete output is retrievable, value survives an output-capture refusal, one envelope cut ledger accounts for output and value. |
| P08 | Printer owners and proof file: bounded lazy stepping and cycle-reference nodes. | `printer-never-counts-an-unknown-tail`: finite unchunked side-effect counter stays at admitted prefix/lookahead bound, infinite tail answers unknown count, cyclic object answers a reference. |
| A phase | Appendix A conversion checkpoints, hook and errors first, then report/REPL/UI owners, then scalar-message producers. | Each row names its exact proposed var and data assertion. Complete source and all same-owner callers are converted in one loadable slice. |
| B phase | Appendix B checkpoints, structural sort/hash first, then logical copies with schema+consumers, delete generic print helpers last. | Equal logical maps yield equal digests independent of order/print bindings; distinct storage values stay distinct; stored logical fields pull back as native data; observational text equals the printer artifact. |
| E01 | `.clj-kondo/config.edn`, `.clj-kondo/hooks/is_message.clj`, existing analyzer owner, proof file: resolved printers and display sinks, error-level rules. | `printer-rule-refuses-bare-and-aliased-printers`: table of direct, alias, higher-order and helper calls yields rule+span data; literal and codec cases pass. |
| E02 | Analyzer/hook owners and proof file: propagation, cuts, stacks, globals and logical copy rules. | `printer-rule-refuses-hidden-cuts-and-copies`: table covers every P2–P8 pattern, including aliases, locals, StringBuilder and ignored findings. |
| E03 | `bin/seon-hook`, `bin/lane`, analyzer owner, hook config and proof file: prospective, post-edit, BB and candidate wiring. | `printer-law-cannot-be-bypassed-by-a-bb-or-shell-edit`: fixture edits are refused with the same finding data at each entrance; no mutation reaches default. |
| E04 | Analyzer owner and proof file: allow only reviewed class-(c) boundaries and test the negative cases. | `printer-rule-keeps-query-and-transport-semantics`: offset parsing, explicit page/deadline, JSON/URL codec and literal ellipsis pass; identical operations feeding a display sink refuse. |

Contracts tighten at the producer: renderer results are data/Hiccup, copy fields have native types, cut counts cannot claim a total for an unknown tail, and diagnostic output cannot bypass the one allocation. Source/contract resource and all consumers publish/adopt together. Existing printer owner tests remain useful, but the above entrance proofs are required before claiming the conversion reaches agents. After targeted proofs, root performs the broad central tier once.

## Landing evidence and limits

Initial `bin/seon status` succeeded as an operator command while reporting default `:faulted`: five open faults, 35 errored receipts, four then five over-bound warnings and 19 unowned namespaces. Known task identities included full-source-refresh `253286d36d52`, `5dbb7de36bdb`, `b55f646ea1c5`; SCI base derivation `4a738d4f7138`; transaction fault `8ab559348c1a`. These observations are not a healthy-runtime pass. Initial live heap used 1,039,816,408 bytes, live 687,494,872, max 10,997,465,088; later operator status saw used 745,567,000, live 703,623,960. They are process observations, not this audit's retained heap or allocation measurement.

Two read-only JVM-mode MCP forms, explicit root/default, defined nothing:

```clojure
{:loaded (into {} (map (fn [s] [s (boolean (find-var s))])
                      '[seon.render.value/prepare seon.render.value/core-text
                        seon.print/emit-both seon.id/digest]))
 :printer (select-keys
           (seon.render.value/prepare
            {:seon.render/value {:a [1 2 3] :b "hello"}
             :seon.render.value/root 'result/printer-audit
             :seon.render.value/options {:seon.render.value/structural? true}})
           [:seon.render.value/truncated? :seon.render.value/elisions
            :seon.render.value/diagnostics :seon.render.value/tree])}
```

Result `result/ea44290f66abe`, 4 ms: four installed Vars true, `truncated? false`, `elisions 0`, map/vector/string tree, no diagnostic. The supplied root is a synthetic identity **for selection only**, not a bound result handle: this probe does not prove runnable continuations.

```clojure
(let [v (apply str (repeat 10000 "x"))
      p (seon.render.value/prepare
         {:seon.render/value v :seon.render.value/root 'result/printer-audit
          :seon.render.value/options {:seon.render.value/structural? true}})]
  {:original-length (count v) :cuts (:seon.render.value/elisions p)
   :truncated? (:seon.render.value/truncated? p)
   :tree (:seon.render.value/tree p)
   :diagnostics (:seon.render.value/diagnostics p)})
```

Result `result/eb1569bc5e7a1`, 3 ms: original length 10000, one cut, offset 4759, omitted 5241, total 10000, no diagnostic. The outer MCP reply independently cut the large `:prefix` field of this **new probe result**, correctly distinguishing the outer result from the inspected inner projection. Estimated displayed sizes: first probe about 600 tokens, second about 1,400 tokens. These finite probes do not prove arbitrary laziness, Throwable totality, HTML painting or stored cut retrieval.

Required working-tree lint: `clj-kondo --lint src script test --cache false --config '{:output {:format :json}}'`: **0 errors, 1455 warnings, 14 info, 506 files, 8033 ms**. Of the warnings, 576 carried the one-printer message. Exit 2 reflects warnings, not errors. This eight-second full source scan is proportional to 506 files and is a gate/census measurement, not an ordinary REPL operation. No dependency-cache refresh or foreign edits were needed.

Foreign boundary: at entry other lanes had edits in `src/seon/render/value.clj`, `src/seon/repl.clj`, `src/seon/sci/eval.clj`, `src/seon/turn.clj`, `src/seon/turn/batch.clj`, associated eval/repl/value schema EDN, `test/seon/mcp_entrance_test.clj`, transcript/value/differential tests, and unrelated research/memory files. This audit changes none. The lane candidate is HEAD plus this document only. No claim that those foreign edits are installed or proven is made.

Existing task families already cover the major defects: `seon.error/error-messages-print-growing-values`, `seon.error/error-admission-is-a-second-clipper`, `seon.error.refusal/errors-capture-whole-stack-traces`, `seon.fault/log-lines-print-values-outside-the-printer`, `seon.render.web/pages-print-values-instead-of-the-printer-html-face`, `seon.program/values-printed-to-order-or-compare`, `seon.operator/operator-prints-whole-results`. Keep the historical task identity for the old `seon.print.value` printed-copy/ad-hoc-clip tasks; do not revive that namespace. New hook enforcement and exact-cut requirements are named by the proposed proof vars above; this read-only audit did not mint live task identities or edit the issue schedule.

The historical source-display landing reports two `seon.render.value-test` failures on its then-HEAD. They are not rerun or asserted current here; no broad printer namespace is passed off as green. This document's only required proof is `seon.contracts-compile-test/a-check-of-no-sources-has-no-contract-findings`; it checks the documentation/no-source boundary, not the designed printer behavior. Production source/test delta is 0/0. The first mandatory lane gate answered `SEON check OK`, total 113,453 ms. Its returned test receipt was run `cfc294a2a3e1`: executed 1, reused 0, pass 2, fail 0, error 0, 1,752 ms. The receipt reported publication 4,773 ms, adoption 1,340 ms, 328 adopted identities, archive 1,442 ms, rehearsal 6,244 ms, and candidate source SHA `daecaf793a700f11a969561f4d23f344d92a5476`. These are the gate's returned timings, not independent measurements of a new default installation. The full candidate check is explicitly requested by the owner; its archive/publication/boot/test cost is separate from the 3–4 ms read-only probes. The test's 1,752 ms is above the ordinary-operation target and remains visible here, not relabeled sub-second.

After proving for 114 seconds, `bin/lane` refused the commit because the foreign `test/seon/sci/acquisition_attributes_test.clj` was staged in the shared index. The audit did not unstage it, edit it or contact its lane. A subsequent `bin/lane commit` must perform the ordinary gate again before landing this finalized document. Git and the successful lane receipt are the final commit authority. No amend.

## Conversion proof tables and exact slice ownership

There are 143 class-(a) and 52 class-(b) conversion checkpoints below, followed by the four enforcement checkpoints in the main plan. This is deliberately larger than a handful of sweeping commits: 1,053 violating line-sites occur in 563 file/function/class groups. A checkpoint takes at most four neighboring producer functions in one file; split it again when contracts or a storage migration exceed the timebox. This is a scheduling estimate, not measured implementation time. Repeated proof names mean extending one behavior-class table, **not** creating hundreds of mirrored tests.

Every conversion checkpoint also owns `test/seon/mcp_entrance_test.clj`; therefore their test-file edits serialize. For an admitted producer's changed data shape, it also owns the schema EDN named in its row and the direct consumers of that changed shape. If a schema/consumer spans another checkpoint, combine that loadable migration or stop at a design/probe checkpoint—never land half the migration. Function names and lines below identify the exact starting slice. The ledger supplies all its call sites and consumers.

Proof vars below live in `seon.mcp-entrance-test` and are proposed, not installed:

| Proof var | DATA assertion through the real MCP entrance |
|---|---|
| `printer-hook-feedback-keeps-findings` | Run an edit-hook fixture under a disposable root from the test world, parse its JSON response as data, and retrieve the report via MCP. Every original finding is retained; the one printer's cut ledger reconstructs hidden findings/source; a direct truncation marker is absent from the result data. |
| `printer-errors-retain-native-members` | Table-call the row's error-producing functions with a long/nested sentinel. The returned/thrown error has a fixed message and native offending/expected/context members or declared reference; the held value survives display; follow each printer continuation via a second MCP call. |
| `printer-renderers-return-semantic-data` | Call each row's AI/HTML render owner with an admitted fixture. Assert its field values/identities or requery data, not preprinted scalar copies; oversized collection members stay available through the held render result; AI and HTML use identical selected cut ids. |
| `printer-test-reports-share-one-selection` | Cause one controlled failing assertion in the disposable test branch and inspect the stored report via MCP: complete expected/actual/message evidence, one report selection, shared budget and exact continuation data. No assertion over formatted report strings. |
| `printer-source-keeps-the-authored-text` | Submit a long definition through MCP in the test world, retrieve the retained source, and reconstruct every cut with the offered continuation. Source equals the authored bytes and no external snippet field destroys its suffix. |
| `printer-operator-and-bridge-return-data` | Run the row's CLI/bridge failure fixture in a disposable root. Decode its protocol response and inspect native fields via MCP; with no DB its one-printer tree accounts for every diagnostic/cut and says explicitly when retrieval is unavailable. |
| `printer-identity-is-structural` | Feed equal reordered maps, unequal structured keys and values under different print bindings through the row's owner via MCP. Equality/digests are stable, unequal native values remain distinct, and ordering uses the declared structural/native key. Check values, never printed keys. |
| `printer-logical-storage-keeps-data` | Exercise the row's canonical writer on the test branch via MCP, pull the fact back and compare the native field with the original value; observational copies instead equal the single retained printer artifact and its ids. Publish/adopt changed schema+consumers together before the pull assertion. |

The assertion hook and structural rule tests additionally inspect analyzer finding maps through MCP. Command execution is only an injected external effect; it is not a second evaluator or test runner.

## Full census ledger

Each row is one counted physical line-site. **What it does** is the exact source fragment and its containing owner; **consumer** names the immediate subsystem. The class and reason describe the particular operation, not an exemption for its entire file. A literal-only or paired-transport row stays class (c) even in a function containing class-(a)/(b) defects. The final column reports the captured-source lint result: `warning` never refuses the edit, and `none` on (a)/(b) is a miss. When an existing helper delegates to `render.value`, its name `printed` alone is not a second printer.

## Census reproduction and completeness checks

Enumerate Clojure/CLJC/CLJS files under `src` and `script` plus BB-shebang files under `bin`. Mask comments, docstrings/string literals and character literals while retaining newlines. Collect resolved or lexical calls/references for core printers, pprint, string conversion/join/format, Java writers/stack/string methods, substring/prefix operations, print globals and higher-order printing keys. Add the named budget helpers and executable elision constructors listed in the ledger; read counts and caps in their containing functions. Deduplicate by `(file,line)` and exclude the two printer owners. Classify by the actual consumer: displayed value, logical printed copy, or a specific paired non-display operation. Inspect literal-only forms so ordinary prose remains allowed. Reconcile the earlier inventory by exact current source, never old line numbers. Recount the ledger and require a conversion checkpoint for every (a)/(b) row.

The known limitation is dynamic resolution and macro-generated behavior not visible in first-party source. That is why the proposed enforcement tracks resolved usages and helper effects and why the implementation proofs go through MCP. A static census is not an installed totality or performance proof.
